<a id='question-100'></a>

### Question 100: Waldeyer's Ring of Lymphoid Tissue: Pharyngeal Tonsil (Adenoids)
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identify the anatomical location and relationship of the pharyngeal tonsil (adenoids) within Waldeyer's ring and its potential impact on the nasopharynx and Eustachian tube opening. (识别咽扁桃体（腺样体）在瓦尔德耶尔淋巴环中的解剖位置及其对鼻咽部和咽鼓管开口的潜在影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the pediatric clinic with a history of recurrent otitis media, chronic nasal congestion, and mouth breathing. Physical examination reveals significant nasal obstruction and a large, erythematous mass filling the posterior nasal cavity, visible upon anterior rhinoscopy. The mass appears to be located superior to the soft palate and posterior to the choanae. The patient's mother reports that he frequently snores and sometimes pauses in his breathing during sleep. An adenoidectomy is planned. During the surgical procedure, the surgeon carefully dissects the mass from the underlying nasopharyngeal mucosa, taking care to avoid injury to the opening of the Eustachian tube located anteriorly and inferiorly.

Which of the following structures is most likely located immediately posterior to the pharyngeal tonsil (adenoids) described in this patient?

(A) The superior constrictor muscle of the pharynx
(B) The fovea palatina
(C) The torus tubarius
(D) The inferior nasal concha
(E) The pterygoid venous plexus

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Anatomical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 6-year-old boy with recurrent otitis media, nasal congestion, mouth breathing, snoring, and sleep apnea symptoms. These are classic signs of adenoid hypertrophy.
- **Physical Examination:** Large, erythematous mass in the posterior nasal cavity, superior to the soft palate, posterior to the choanae. This confirms adenoid hypertrophy.
- **Surgical Context:** Adenoidectomy planned, emphasizing dissection from the nasopharyngeal mucosa and avoiding the Eustachian tube opening (anterior and inferior).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the structure immediately posterior to the pharyngeal tonsil (adenoids).

##### 2. Anatomical, Embryological & Clinical Deep-Dive (解剖毗邻、神经血管支配、胚胎发生与临床相关性深度剖析)
- **Pharyngeal Tonsil (Adenoids):** Located in the roof and posterior wall of the nasopharynx, superior to the soft palate. It is part of Waldeyer's ring.
- **Anatomical Relationships:**
    - **Anterior:** Posterior nasal choanae, opening of the Eustachian tube (auditory tube).
    - **Inferior:** Soft palate.
    - **Lateral:** Opening of the Eustachian tube, pharyngeal recess (fovea palatina).
    - **Posterior:** Posterior wall of the nasopharynx, superior constrictor muscle.
    - **Superior:** Base of the skull (sphenoid and occipital bones).
- **Torus Tubarius:** A cartilaginous elevation located on the posterior wall of the nasopharynx, just posterior and slightly inferior to the opening of the Eustachian tube. It is formed by the cartilage of the Eustachian tube. The pharyngeal tonsil (adenoids) lies anterior and superior to the torus tubarius. Therefore, the torus tubarius is located immediately posterior to the adenoids.
- **Superior Constrictor Muscle:** Forms the posterior wall of the nasopharynx, posterior to the adenoids, but the torus tubarius is more immediately posterior and slightly inferior.
- **Fovea Palatina:** A depression on the posterior wall of the nasopharynx, located inferior to the torus tubarius and posterior to the pharyngeal recess.
- **Inferior Nasal Concha:** Located within the nasal cavity, inferior to the middle nasal concha, not in the nasopharynx.
- **Pterygoid Venous Plexus:** Located in the infratemporal fossa, lateral to the mandible, far from the nasopharynx.

##### 3. Embryological Origin:** Waldeyer's ring, including the pharyngeal tonsil, develops from lymphoid tissue in the pharyngeal mucosa. The pharyngeal tonsil specifically arises from the lymphoid tissue in the roof of the nasopharynx.

##### 4. Clinical Correlation:** Adenoid hypertrophy obstructs the nasopharynx, leading to nasal obstruction, mouth breathing, snoring, and sleep apnea. It can also obstruct the Eustachian tube opening, leading to Eustachian tube dysfunction and recurrent otitis media.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **The superior constrictor muscle of the pharynx:** This muscle forms the posterior wall of the nasopharynx, lying posterior to the adenoids. However, the torus tubarius is located more immediately posterior and slightly inferior to the adenoids, directly adjacent to the Eustachian tube opening. The adenoids are anterior to the superior constrictor.
- (B) **The fovea palatina:** This is a depression on the posterior wall of the nasopharynx, located inferior to the torus tubarius. The adenoids are superior and anterior to the fovea palatina.
- (D) **The inferior nasal concha:** This structure is located within the nasal cavity, inferior to the middle nasal concha, and is not directly related to the posterior nasopharynx where the adenoids reside.
- (E) **The pterygoid venous plexus:** This plexus is located in the infratemporal fossa, lateral to the mandible, far removed from the nasopharynx and the adenoids.

#### High-Yield Anatomical Comparison Table (重点解剖结构与易混淆点横向对比):
| Structure                 | Location Relative to Adenoids (Pharyngeal Tonsil) | Key Features                                                                 |
| :------------------------ | :---------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Torus Tubarius**        | **Immediately Posterior**                       | Cartilaginous elevation, posterior wall of nasopharynx, near Eustachian tube |
| Superior Constrictor Muscle | Posterior                                       | Forms posterior wall of nasopharynx                                           |
| Fovea Palatina            | Inferior and Posterior                          | Depression on posterior wall of nasopharynx                                   |
| Inferior Nasal Concha     | Within Nasal Cavity (Inferior)                  | Not in nasopharynx                                                            |
| Pterygoid Venous Plexus   | Infratemporal Fossa (Lateral)                   | Not in nasopharynx                                                            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The pharyngeal tonsil (adenoids) is located in the roof and posterior wall of the nasopharynx, anterior to the torus tubarius and superior constrictor muscle. Understanding its anatomical relationships is crucial for surgical procedures like adenoidectomy and for understanding the pathophysiology of related conditions like obstructive sleep apnea and otitis media. Key differentials include the location of the Eustachian tube opening (anterior/inferior), the torus tubarius (posterior), and the superior constrictor muscle (posterior wall).
